<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every intersection of parabolic subgroups of any finite-rank Artin group, with arbitrary finite or infinite Coxeter labels, is parabolic. This resolves the Parabolic Intersection Conjecture affirmatively.

- **claim-parabolic-intersection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every Coxeter matrix $M$ on a finite set $S$, subsets $X,Y\subseteq S$, and $g,h\in A_M$, there are $Z\subseteq S$ and $k\in A_M$ such that $$gA_Xg^{-1}\cap hA_Yh^{-1}=kA_Zk^{-1}.$$
  - Explanation: Any two parabolic subgroups of a finite-rank Artin group intersect in a parabolic subgroup, for arbitrary finite or infinite Coxeter labels.
- **claim-arbitrary-intersections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-arbitrary-intersections)): For every family $\mathcal F$ of parabolic subgroups of $A$, there is a subfamily $\mathcal F_0\subseteq\mathcal F$ with $\|\mathcal F_0\|\le \|S\|$ such that $$\bigcap_{P\in\mathcal F}P=\bigcap_{P\in\mathcal F_0}P.$$ Here the intersection of an empty family is $A$. In particular, arbitrary intersections of parabolic subgroups are parabolic, and every subset $E\subseteq A$ is contained in a unique smallest parabolic subgroup.
  - Explanation: An arbitrary intersection of parabolic subgroups is already realised by a subfamily whose size is bounded by the rank, so it is parabolic and parabolic closure exists.
- **claim-membership-algorithm** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cor-membership)): There is an algorithm which, given a finite Coxeter matrix $M$, a subset $X\subseteq S$, and words $u,v$ in the standard generators of $A_M$ and their inverses, decides whether the element represented by $v$ belongs to $uA_Xu^{-1}$. In particular, the word problem is decidable for every finite-rank Artin group.
  - Explanation: A uniform procedure decides membership of a word in a conjugate of a standard parabolic subgroup, hence also solves the word problem.
- **claim-nonclique-dynamics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-nonclique-dynamics)): Suppose $M$ is irreducible, meaning that there is no nontrivial partition $S=S_1\sqcup S_2$ with $m_{st}=2$ for every $s\in S_1$, $t\in S_2$, and suppose $m_{st}=\infty$ for some pair. Then $A$ is acylindrically hyperbolic. Every proper parabolic subgroup $P$ has a conjugate meeting it trivially: there is $g\in A$ such that $P\cap gPg^{-1}=\{1\}$.
  - Explanation: For irreducible matrices with some infinite label the group is acylindrically hyperbolic and every proper parabolic subgroup has a conjugate meeting it trivially.
- **claim-classical-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-classical)): The group defined by the Artin presentation on $T\subseteq S$ embeds in $A$, with image $A_T$. No sphericity hypothesis is needed here. For a spherical $T$, the positive Artin monoid $A_T^+$ embeds in $A_T$, is cancellative, and has greatest common divisors on both sides. Every element of $A_T$ is both a left fraction and a right fraction of positive elements. Any two elements $a,b\in A_T$ thus have a common positive extension $pa=qb$, with $p,q\in A_T^+$.* *Reduced expressions in any Coxeter system are related by braid moves. They therefore define a positive lift $\iota(w)$ of a Coxeter element $w$. If $T$ is spherical and $w_T$ is its longest element, the left and right divisors of $\Delta_T=\iota(w_T)$ are precisely $\{\iota(w):w\in W_T\}$. In the finite geometric representation, root signs detect descents, the group acts simply transitively on chambers, and the translates of a closed fundamental chamber cover the entire dual space.
  - Explanation: Recalls the classical embedding of standard parabolic subgroups and the monoid, divisor and chamber facts for spherical subsystems.
- **claim-cartan-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-cartan)): For spherical $T$, the inverse $C_T^{-1}$ is nonnegative, and is strictly positive in each connected block. Prescribed exterior coordinates consequently have a unique extension $p$ satisfying $(Cp)_T=0$. This extension is nonnegative if the prescribed coordinates are nonnegative.* *If $G$ is nonempty and connected, $v_G>0$, and $C_Gv_G\geq0$, then either $C_G$ is positive definite or $C_Gv_G=0$.
  - Explanation: On spherical sets the inverse Cartan matrix is nonnegative and blockwise positive, giving harmonic extensions, and a positive vector with nonnegative image forces positive definiteness or a null vector.
- **claim-frobenius-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-frobenius)): The functional $$\tau(t_{i,L})=w_L, \qquad \tau(Z^0\oplus Z^1)=0$$ is a symmetric Frobenius trace of degree $-2$. Opposite arrow spaces pair perfectly by multiplication followed by $\tau$. The radical of $Z$ is $Z^{>0}$, and the socle of every indecomposable left or right projective is its token line.* *For a finite graded bimodule $N$, its adjoint dual satisfies $$N^\vee:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{Z\text{-left}}(N,Z) \cong D_\mathbb C(N)\langle2\rangle, \qquad (P_\alpha\otimes_\mathbb CQ_\beta)^\vee \cong(P_\beta\otimes_\mathbb CQ_\alpha)\langle-2\rangle. \label{cat:adjoint-dual}$$ This is an involutive contravariant equivalence, fixes the regular bimodule, and preserves additive radicals.
  - Explanation: The functional on the zigzag algebra is a symmetric Frobenius trace giving perfect pairings, radical and socle descriptions and an adjoint duality on bimodules.
- **claim-finite-unfolding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-unfolding)): For spherical $T\subseteq S$, the matrix $\widetilde C_{\widetilde T}$ is positive definite and simply laced. Replacing an original reflection $s_i$ by the product of the commuting reflections in its color gives a homomorphism from $W_T$ to the resulting finite Coxeter group. Under this homomorphism, $$D_L(\widetilde w)=\widetilde{D_L(w)}, \qquad D_R(\widetilde w)=\widetilde{D_R(w)},$$ where $D_L$ and $D_R$ are the left and right descent sets. Every reduced expression lifts to a reduced expression. In particular, the longest element lifts to the longest element.
  - Explanation: Unfolding along labels gives a positive definite simply laced matrix and a descent-preserving homomorphism of Coxeter groups lifting reduced words.
- **claim-categorical-action** (construction, [source](#stmt-cat-action)): There are bimodule chain homotopy equivalences $B_\alpha D_\alpha\simeq Z\simeq D_\alpha B_\alpha$. For a color $i\in S$, define $$B_i=\prod_{\alpha\in\widetilde{\{i\}}}B_\alpha, \qquad D_i=\prod_{\alpha\in\widetilde{\{i\}}}D_\alpha.$$ These products are independent of their order up to bimodule homotopy, are mutually inverse, and satisfy all the defining Artin relations. They define an action of $A$ by invertible graded bimodule homotopy classes, denoted $B_x$ for $x\in A$. In particular, $$F_i(E):=B_i\otimes_ZE =\mathop{\mathrm{Cone}}\left( \bigoplus_{\alpha\in\widetilde{\{i\}}} P_\alpha\otimes_\mathbb Ce_\alpha E\longrightarrow E\right). \label{cat:color-evaluation}$$ Every $B_x$ preserves bounded projective complexes and preserves the cohomology of their bigraded Hom complexes. Its adjoint dual $B_x^\vee$ represents its inverse. All the action identities are actual bimodule chain homotopy equivalences and remain valid after tensoring with any finite module complex, whether or not projective.* *For $H\in\mathscr C$, right tensoring of labels defines an additive functor $$T_H(P_{i,D})=P_{i,D\otimes H}$$ on graded projectives. It preserves path lengths, extends to bounded homotopy categories, and commutes naturally up to homotopy with $B_x\otimes_Z-$ for every $x\in A$.
  - Explanation: Evaluation and coevaluation twists are mutually inverse bimodule homotopy equivalences satisfying the Artin relations, giving an action on bounded projective complexes compatible with label functors.
- **claim-family-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-family)): There are nonnegative folded vectors $m^d(x)$ characterized by $$\phi m^d(x)=\sum_Lw_L\dim Y^d(E_L(x)). \label{cat:folded-counts}$$ They have finite level support. Each individual coordinate ranges over a locally finite subset of $\mathbb R_{\geq0}$. Weighted counts of tops and socles of the layers are likewise proportional within a color: a nonzero top or socle in that color occurs at every unfolded vertex of the color in some member of the family. The same proportionality holds for families of subobjects obtained functorially by twists and layer truncations.* *If only colors of $T\subseteq S$ act, all generator spaces with their cochain and internal placements outside $\widetilde T$ stay unchanged up to isomorphism. In particular, each $E_L(x)$ has exactly its original $f$-generator in placements $(0,0)$, and $$m_f^0(x)=1,\qquad m_f^d(x)=0\quad(d\ne0).$$ Every $E_L(x)$ is indecomposable in the graded homotopy category. The color support of its minimal complex is connected and contains $f$.
  - Explanation: The transported family has folded count vectors with finite support and local finiteness, is unchanged outside the acting colors, and consists of indecomposables with connected support.
- **claim-layer-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-structure)): The full subcategories defined by minimal generator levels at most $0$ and at least $0$ form a bounded $t$-structure. The truncation $\tau_{\le k}E$ is the subcomplex of levels at most $k$, and the complementary quotient is $\tau_{\ge k+1}E$.* *The heart is the abelian category of finite graded coefficient representations. In particular, subobjects of a pure layer are exactly its graded subrepresentations. Its unshifted cohomology layer at $d$ is $Y^d(E)$.
  - Explanation: Generator levels define a bounded t-structure whose heart is the category of finite graded coefficient representations.
- **claim-adjacent-layers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-adjacent)): For an exact functor $F$ of layer amplitude $[0,1]$ there is a short exact sequence of pure level-$d$ objects $$\begin{split} 0\longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^{d-1}(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow Y^d(FE) \longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^d(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow0. \end{split}\label{lay:adjacent-positive}$$ For amplitude $[-1,0]$ the corresponding sequence is $$\begin{split} 0\longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^d(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow Y^d(FE) \longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^{d+1}(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow0. \end{split}\label{lay:adjacent-negative}$$ All terms are shifted by $[d]$ to regard these as sequences in the heart. In particular, generator dimensions are additive.
  - Explanation: A functor of layer amplitude between the stated bounds yields short exact sequences relating one output layer to adjacent input layers, so generator dimensions add.
- **claim-bottom-composition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-bottom)): Let $F_1,\ldots,F_r$ be exact functors with no negative change of level. On input pure at $d$, the level-$d$ part of $F_r\cdots F_1$ is obtained by retaining only level $d$ after every operation. Relations between these composite functors induce the same relations between their bottom-layer operations.
  - Explanation: For composites of functors that never lower level, the bottom layer is computed by keeping only that level after each step, and relations descend to bottom operations.
- **claim-extremal-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-extremal)): If $M$ is pure at level $d$, then $$H^d(e_\alpha M)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{soc}}_\alpha M, \qquad H^{d+2}(e_\alpha M)\cong(\mathop{\mathrm{top}}_\alpha M)\langle2\rangle. \label{lay:extremal-formula}$$ The second shift records the token’s internal placement. More generally, if a minimal complex $E$ has no generators above $d$ at $\alpha$ or its neighbors, its degree-$(d+2)$ test is the top of $Y^d(E)$ at $\alpha$, shifted internally by $2$, and all higher test cohomology there vanishes. If there are no generators below $d$ at those vertices, its degree-$d$ test is the socle of $Y^d(E)$ there.* *A nonzero pure layer supported on a vertex set has a nonzero top and a nonzero socle somewhere in that set. The same assertions hold for right projective complexes, tested by $Me_\alpha$.
  - Explanation: For a pure layer the extremal test cohomologies are the socle and the shifted top, and a nonzero layer has a nonzero top and socle.
- **claim-positive-layers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-positive)): Each $F_i$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$. For a pure input $M$ at $d$, its upper layer is supported on color $i$, has zero linear differential, and has multiplicity $\dim\mathop{\mathrm{top}}_\alpha M$ at each $\alpha$ of that color. Its bottom layer has unchanged multiplicities at all other vertices, and $$\dim Y^d(F_iM)_\alpha =\sum_\beta(2I-\widetilde C)_{\alpha\beta}\dim M_\beta -\dim M_\alpha+\dim\mathop{\mathrm{top}}_\alpha M \quad(\alpha\text{ of color }i). \label{lay:positive-count}$$ The bottom layer has zero socle in color $i$. If $i\in J$ and $M$ has zero socle on every color of $J$, its bottom layer after $F_i$ has zero socle on all of $J$.* *For any bounded complex $E$ of graded left modules, with no projectivity requirement on its terms, $$e_\alpha F_iE\simeq(e_\alpha E)\langle2\rangle[1] \quad(\alpha\text{ of color }i). \label{lay:positive-shift}$$ There is an identical homotopy equivalence for right actions and right tests. Thus an acted test shifts up one in total degree and down one in cochain degree.
  - Explanation: Color twists have layer amplitude between zero and one, with an explicit multiplicity formula, vanishing socle in the acted color, and a test shift for arbitrary complexes.
- **claim-inverse-amplitude** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-inverse)): An exact equivalence of layer amplitude $[0,1]$ has inverse amplitude $[-1,0]$. In particular $F_i^{-1}$ has that amplitude, and $$e_\alpha F_i^{-1}E\simeq(e_\alpha E)\langle-2\rangle[-1] \quad(\alpha\text{ of color }i). \label{lay:negative-shift}$$ This test identity is valid for arbitrary bounded module complexes and also on the right. For either sign, acting in color $i$ leaves the minimal generator spaces at other colors unchanged up to isomorphism, with both placements retained. Formula <a href="#lay:signed-reflection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lay:signed-reflection">[lay:signed-reflection]</a> also holds for $F_i^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: The inverse of an amplitude-nonnegative equivalence has nonpositive amplitude, with the corresponding test shift and invariance at other colors.
- **claim-label-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-labels)): Layer truncations commute with the exact label functors $T_H$. For the family $E_L(x)$ of Proposition <a href="#cat:family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cat:family">10</a>, the weighted generator, top and socle dimensions in each layer are proportional on every color fiber. In particular, a top or socle occurring in one member at a vertex of color $i$ occurs at every vertex of color $i$ in some member of the family. The same proportionality holds for families constructed from this one by twists and layer truncations.
  - Explanation: Label tensoring commutes with truncations and keeps generator, top and socle dimensions proportional along each color fiber.
- **claim-height-change** (bound, [source](#stmt-lay-height-change)): If $h_J(E)$ is finite, then $$h_J(E)\le h_J(B_\alpha E)\le h_J(E)+1.$$ Height increases exactly when $\alpha$ was a highest-test vertex; in that case it is the only new highest-test vertex. Vanishing of all tests on $\widetilde J$ persists. The same statements hold for right actions. Consequently height is nondecreasing under positive color words in $J$.
  - Explanation: A single twist raises the test height by at most one, exactly when acting at a highest-test vertex, so height is nondecreasing under positive words.
- **claim-top-divisor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lay-top-divisor)): Let $b$ be a positive word in the unfolded finite-type generators. If $h_J(B_bE)>h_J(E)$, every vertex attaining the highest test cohomology of $B_bE$ is a left divisor of $b$ in the positive Artin monoid. For a right action, such a vertex is a right divisor of $b$. These conclusions hold for arbitrary inputs with bounded test cohomology, including right-simple complexes.
  - Explanation: If a positive word raises test height, every highest-test vertex of the result divides the word on the side of the last action, even for non-projective inputs.
- **claim-reduced-amplitude** (bound, [source](#stmt-lay-reduced)): For a spherical set $J$, the positive lift of a reduced Coxeter word has layer amplitude $[0,1]$. The negative lift, obtained by replacing each letter by its inverse without reversing the word, has amplitude $[-1,0]$. These bounds hold on every pure projective input, and hence on arbitrary bounded intervals of layers, on either module side.
  - Explanation: Positive lifts of reduced Coxeter words have layer amplitude between zero and one, and negative lifts between minus one and zero.
- **claim-capped-negative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-capped-negative)): Let $E$ be a bounded projective complex capped at $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$, and let $G$ be any word of negative twists in $J$. Then $GE$ has the same cap, and $$Y^q(GE)\simeq Y^q\bigl(G(Y^q(E))\bigr). \label{cap:negative-layer}$$
  - Explanation: Negative twists inside J preserve a joint cap and act on the top layer only through that layer.
- **claim-cap-accessibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-access)): Let $J\subseteq S$ be spherical and suppose $A_Jx$ has a cap at $q$ on $\partial J$. It has a representative capped at $q$ also on $J$. Every negative letter in $J$ preserves this joint cap. A positive letter in $J$ preserves it whenever the top at that color in the level-$q$ layer vanishes in every family member.
  - Explanation: A coset with a cap on the boundary has a representative capped also on J; negative letters preserve the cap and positive letters do so when the relevant top vanishes.
- **claim-two-point-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-uniqueness)): Let $T\subseteq S$ be nonempty and spherical, let $x_0\in A$, and let $q\in\mathbb Z$ cap $(E_L(x_0))_L$ on $\partial T$. There is at most one typed coset $(J,A_Jz)$ satisfying Definition <a href="#two:definition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="two:definition">25</a> in $A_Tx_0$ at level $q$.
  - Explanation: Within a capped spherical coset at a given level there is at most one typed coset satisfying the candidate conditions.
- **claim-capped-hull** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-hull)): Suppose $x$ is capped at $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$, with $J$ spherical. Let $p$ be the harmonic interpolation of $m^q(x)$ on $J$. Assume $$m_j^q(y)\ge p_j\quad(j\in J) \quad\text{for every jointly capped }y\in A_Jx. \label{cap:universal}$$ Put $e=m^q(x)-p$, which is supported on $J$. Then $e_J$ belongs to the convex hull of finitely many vectors $s_J$ for which $s$ is supported on $J$ and there are graded subrepresentations $$\mathcal S_L\subseteq Y^q(E_L(x)),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mathcal S_L\subseteq\widetilde J,\qquad \sum_Lw_L\dim\mathcal S_L=\phi s. \label{cap:subobjects}$$ If every inequality in <a href="#cap:universal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cap:universal">[cap:universal]</a> is strict, $e_J$ is in the ordinary interior of such a hull in $\mathbb R^J$. For $J=\varnothing$ this uses the convention that $\{0\}$ is open in $\mathbb R^0$.
  - Explanation: The excess of a capped layer over its harmonic interpolation lies in a convex hull of dimension vectors of subrepresentations, in the interior under strict inequalities.
- **claim-right-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-duality)): For all $\ell,\beta\in\widetilde T$ and $r\in\mathbb Z$, $$K^r_{\ell\beta}(h)=K^{-2-r}_{\beta\ell}(h^{-1}). \label{two:duality-equation}$$ For $J,J'\subseteq T$, left multiplication of $h$ by $A_{J'}$ leaves all rows outside $\widetilde J'$ unchanged. Right multiplication by $A_J$ leaves all columns outside $\widetilde J$ unchanged.
  - Explanation: Generator counts of a transported right simple satisfy a duality exchanging an element with its inverse, with invariance of rows and columns under parabolic multiplication.
- **claim-greedy-divisibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-greedy)): For nonidentity simple factors $d_1,\ldots,d_k$, repeated extraction of the greatest simple suffix gives $d_1\cdots d_k$ precisely when $$D_R(d_i)\subseteq D_L(d_{i+1})\qquad(1\le i<k). \label{two:greedy-condition}$$ If an unfolded vertex right-divides the unfolding of a positive element of $A_T$, its color right-divides the original element. The analogous assertion holds for left divisors.
  - Explanation: Greedy factorization into simple factors is characterised by a descent condition, and divisibility by an unfolded vertex descends to divisibility by its color.
- **claim-parabolic-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-detection)): If $\varnothing\ne V\subseteq T$ and $$K^r_{\ell\beta}(h)=0 \qquad(r\in\mathbb Z,\ \ell,\beta\in\widetilde V), \label{two:zero-block}$$ then $h\in A_{T\setminus V}$.
  - Explanation: Vanishing of all generator counts on a block indexed by V forces the group element into the standard parabolic subgroup on the complement.
- **claim-negative-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-negative-bound)): A right inverse twist, followed by minimization, preserves an upper normalized bound $r\ge-1$ for a complex with one residual simple at $(0,0)$ and shifted right projectives. At its operated vertex $\alpha$ the tests satisfy $$(R D_\alpha)e_\alpha \simeq (Re_\alpha)\langle-2\rangle[-1]. \label{two:negative-test}$$ Both assertions hold for a full color twist as well.
  - Explanation: A right inverse twist followed by minimization keeps the upper normalized bound and shifts the test at the operated vertex.
- **claim-highest-tensor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-tensor)): One has $H_2(N,M)=0$, and $H_1(N,M)$ survives in actual total degree $r+q+1$. At any projective vertex the token cokernel surjects onto the tensor product of the right and left top quotients. In particular a highest right top in a color $j\in J$ forces all level-$q$ left-family tops in that color to vanish.
  - Explanation: In the highest tensor pair the top homology vanishes and the middle homology survives in the stated degree, so a highest right top forces vanishing of left tops in that color.
- **claim-remove-right-tops** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-normalization)): Changing $z$ within $A_Jz$, while keeping its cap on $T$, one can arrange that no highest right top occurs on $\widetilde J$.
  - Explanation: Moving the representative inside its coset removes all highest right tops on J while keeping the cap.
- **claim-subobject-tensor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-subobject-tensor)): For each such subfamily, $$H_0(N,\mathcal S)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow H_0(N,M), \qquad H_2(N,\mathcal S)=0.$$
  - Explanation: For a subfamily of the left layers, the lowest homology injects into the ambient one and the top homology vanishes.
- **claim-euler-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-two-euler)): At the normalized representative, $$\frac{\dim_{\mathrm{wt}}H_1(N,M)}{D_0} \ge n_T^{\mathsf T}a+ \mathbf1_{\{r=-1\}}(a')^{\mathsf T}p_T. \label{two:estimate}$$
  - Explanation: A weighted Euler characteristic argument bounds the middle homology of the tensor pair from below at the normalized representative.
- **claim-null-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-null-obstruction)): Let $I\subseteq S$ be nonempty and connected, let $c_I>0$ satisfy $C_Ic_I=0$, and extend $c$ by zero outside $I$. Let $J\subseteq I$ be spherical, possibly empty, let $q\in\mathbb Z$, and fix $A_Jx$. Suppose its fixed data on $(I\setminus J)\cup\partial I$ are capped at $q$ and satisfy $$m^q\big\|_{(I\setminus J)\cup\partial I} =c\big\|_{(I\setminus J)\cup\partial I}. \label{null:exterior}$$ It is impossible that every representative $z\in A_Jx$ capped at $q$ on $I$ satisfies $$m_j^q(z)>c_j\qquad\text{for every }j\in J. \label{null:strict}$$ Thus, if $J\ne\varnothing$, some such representative has $m_j^q(z)\le c_j$ for at least one $j\in J$. If $J=\varnothing$, the exterior hypotheses themselves are impossible.
  - Explanation: For a connected set carrying a positive null vector, it is impossible that every capped representative strictly exceeds the null vector on all of J.
- **claim-hom-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-null-hom)): For pure layers, including layers obtained from graded subrepresentations, there are perfect pairings $$\mathsf H_j^{n,v}(V,W)\ \times\ \mathsf H_{2-j}^{-n,\,2-v}(W,V)\longrightarrow\mathbb C. \label{null:pairing}$$ In particular, $$\begin{split} h_2(V,W)&=h_0(W,V),\\ (\dim V)^{\mathsf T}\widetilde C\,\dim W &=h_0(V,W)+h_0(W,V)-h_1(V,W), \end{split} \label{null:euler}$$ where dimensions count projective generators, summed over placements. For every $z\in A$, every pair of labels $L,H$, and all levels $l,k$, $$h_1\bigl(Y^l(E_L(z)),Y^k(E_H(z))\bigr)=0. \label{null:middle}$$ The vanishing persists after replacing either layer by a direct summand as a pure layer.
  - Explanation: Linear Hom cohomology between pure layers has perfect Frobenius pairings and an Euler identity, and its middle column vanishes for layers of transported family members.
- **claim-secondary-injection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-null-injection)): Suppose the family at $z$ is capped at $q$ on $I\subseteq S$. For each $L$, let $M_L$ be a direct summand of $Y^q(E_L(z))$ as a pure layer, supported on $\widetilde I$, and put $\mathcal M=(M_L)_L$. For $d<q$ there are injections $$\mathsf H_0^{n,v}\bigl(M_L,Y^d(E_H(z))\bigr) \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow \mathsf H_2^{n+1,v}\bigl(M_L,Y^{d-1}(E_H(z))\bigr), \qquad n+v=d-q. \label{null:secondary-map}$$ Consequently, $$h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal M,\mathcal Y^d) \le h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal Y^{d-1},\mathcal M). \label{null:secondary-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Under a cap, length-zero Hom classes inject into length-two classes one layer lower, giving an inequality of weighted dimensions.
- **claim-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-null-separation)): Let $I\subseteq S$ be nonempty and connected, with $c_I>0$ and $C_Ic_I=0$, and extend $c$ by zero. Let $J\subseteq I$. Suppose $\mathcal M=(M_L)_L$ consists of direct summands of a single layer family $\mathcal Y^q$, supported on $\widetilde I$, with weighted generator array $\phi m$. Assume $m-c$ is supported on $J$, and $(m-c)_J$ is an interior point in $\mathbb R^J$ of the convex hull of finitely many vectors $s_J$. Each such $s$ is zero off $J$, and $\phi s$ is the weighted generator array of a family of graded subrepresentations $\mathcal S\subseteq\mathcal M$ supported on $\widetilde J$.* *If the socle of $\mathcal Y^d$ vanishes on $J$, and its folded weighted vector is $y=m^d(z)$, then $$\begin{gathered} y\|_{\partial I}=0,\qquad (Cy)_J=0,\\ h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal M,\mathcal Y^d) =h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal Y^d,\mathcal M)=0. \end{gathered} \label{null:separation-conclusions}$$ The usual zero-dimensional interior convention is used when $J=\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: With an interior hull condition and vanishing socle on J, the layer vector vanishes on the boundary of I, is harmonic on J, and has vanishing mutual Hom with the level-q summand.
- **claim-attaching-neighborhoods** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-vertex-filtration)): Let a poset be well-ordered by an auxiliary order. Suppose that, for each vertex, its earlier neighbors are empty or connected in the graph on earlier vertices. Then each connected component contains exactly one vertex with no earlier neighbor.
  - Explanation: If earlier neighbors of each vertex are empty or connected, each component of the comparability graph has exactly one vertex without earlier neighbors.
- **claim-sublevel-connectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-sublevels)): The strict sublevel is empty or connected. Under Equation <a href="#ht:closed-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ht:closed-data">[ht:closed-data]</a>, the special closed sublevel is nonempty and connected. For $T=\varnothing$, the strict sublevel is empty and the special closed exterior data cannot occur. These statements hold for every integer $q$.
  - Explanation: Strict spherical sublevels are empty or connected, and the special closed sublevel for a null component is nonempty and connected.
- **claim-height-well-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-well-order)): The possible heights are well ordered. Their least block is the zero block.
  - Explanation: The possible harmonic heights are well ordered and the least one is the zero block.
- **claim-upper-neighbors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-upper)): The union of $T$ with all active colors is spherical. The descending upper neighbors of $v$ are empty or connected using only descending upper neighbors.
  - Explanation: Adding all active colors to the type stays spherical, and descending upper neighbors are empty or connected among themselves.
- **claim-lower-neighbors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-lower)): Every descending lower neighbor opens an eligible color. Opening a nonempty set of eligible colors and no others gives a descending neighbor. The descending lower neighbors of $v$ are empty or connected using only descending lower neighbors.
  - Explanation: Descending lower neighbors are exactly those opening eligible colors, and they form an empty or connected set.
- **claim-descending-neighborhoods** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-descending)): The descending neighbors of each vertex are empty or connected in its strict height sublevel.
  - Explanation: Combining upper and lower parts, the descending neighbors of any vertex are empty or connected in the strict sublevel.
- **claim-tie-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-ties)): If $v<v'$ have equal height, then $K(v)=K(v')$ and $T_+(v)=T_+(v')$. There is no adjacency between these common visible and invisible sets. Moreover either both descending neighborhoods are empty, or they are nonempty and belong to the same connected component of their common strict height sublevel.
  - Explanation: Tied comparable vertices share their visible and invisible sets, and their descending neighborhoods lie in one component of the strict sublevel.
- **claim-connected-minima** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-minima)): The minimum-height vertices in $\mathcal P(Y,x)$ form a nonempty connected graph. Their sets $K$ and $T_+$ are constant along this graph.
  - Explanation: Minimum-height vertices form a nonempty connected graph on which the visible and invisible sets are constant.
- **claim-minimum-containment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-containment)): Put $D=T_+\cup K\subseteq Y$. The set $T_+$ is spherical and nonadjacent to $K$, and $$x^{-1}H(Y,x)x\subseteq z^{-1}A_Dz. \label{ht:containment-equation}$$ Equivalently, with $b=zx^{-1}\in A_Y$, $H(Y,x)\subseteq b^{-1}A_Db$.
  - Explanation: The stabilizer is contained in a conjugate of the parabolic subgroup on the visible spherical part together with the invisible part.
- **claim-path-nilpotence** (bound, [source](#stmt-sph-path-bound)): Let $Y\subseteq S$ be spherical. There is an integer $N=N(Y)\geq1$ with the following property. Consider a minimal bounded complex of projectives and an integer interval of cochain placements on which all its generator vertices lie in $\widetilde Y$. In each pure layer, every composition of $N$ successive arrow coefficient maps vanishes, provided all $N+1$ placements of the composition lie in that interval.
  - Explanation: On a spherical set, long compositions of arrow coefficient maps in a pure layer vanish within an interval of placements, with a bound depending only on the set.
- **claim-cochain-drop** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sph-cochain-drop)): Suppose that all tests on $\widetilde Y$ in a bounded family have ordinary cochain cohomology in degrees at most $m$. Positive operations in $Y$ preserve this bound. After a positive word containing every color of $Y$, the bound improves to $m-1$.
  - Explanation: Positive operations keep an upper bound on ordinary cochain test cohomology, and a positive word containing every color lowers it.
- **claim-empty-placement** (bound, [source](#stmt-sph-empty-placement)): Let a minimal bounded projective complex have generator levels in $[d_1,d_2]$. Suppose that on an integer cochain interval $[l,r]$ all its generators lie in $\widetilde Y$, and all vertex tests on $\widetilde Y$ have zero cohomology in each ordinary cochain degree in this interval, in every internal degree. With $N=N(Y)$ from Lemma <a href="#sph:path-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:path-bound">49</a>, there are no generators at any placement $$l+N(d_2-d_1+1)\leq p\leq r.$$
  - Explanation: Vanishing test cohomology on a cochain interval forces an explicit window of placements to contain no generators.
- **claim-no-bounded-chain** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sph-no-chain)): Assume $h(1)>-\infty$. There is no sequence $$a_0=1,\qquad a_{k+1}=w_k a_k,\qquad w_k\in A_Y^+,\quad\operatorname{supp}(w_k)=Y,$$ for which the integers $h(a_k)$ have a common upper bound.
  - Explanation: No infinite chain of fully supported positive words can keep the total test height bounded above.
- **claim-common-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sph-common-extension)): For $a,b\in A_Y$, there are $p,q\in A_Y^+$ and $u\in A_Y$ such that $$u=pa=qb,\qquad h(u)\leq\max(h(a),h(b)).$$ The same upper bound holds for a common positive left extension of any finite family.
  - Explanation: Two spherical elements have a common positive left extension whose test height does not exceed the larger of theirs.
- **claim-proper-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sph-proper-support)): Assume $h(1)>-\infty$, and put $h_0=h(1)$. There exist $p\in A_Y^+$ with $h(p)\leq h_0$ and one proper subset $J\subsetneq Y$ such that $$b\in A_Y^+,\quad h(bp)\leq h_0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \operatorname{supp}(b)\subseteq J.$$
  - Explanation: There is a positive element and a single proper subset of colors containing the support of every height-preserving continuation.
- **claim-spherical-containment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sph-containment)): Let $Y\subseteq S$ be spherical and $x\in A$. For the family stabilizer $$H(Y,x)=\{s\in A_Y:E_L(sx)\simeq E_L(x) \text{ for every }L\},$$ one of the following holds:* 1. *all tests on $\widetilde Y$ at $x$ are acyclic and $H(Y,x)=A_Y$;* 2. *there exist $p\in A_Y^+$ and $J\subsetneq Y$ such that $$\label{sph:eq:containment} H(Y,x)\subseteq p^{-1}A_Jp .$$
  - Explanation: In spherical type the stabilizer is either the whole group, when all tests are acyclic, or sits in a conjugate of a proper standard parabolic.
- **claim-stabilizer-conjugation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stab-conjugation)): Let $D\subseteq Y\subseteq S$ and $b\in A_Y$. If $H(Y,x)\subseteq b^{-1}A_Db$, then $$bH(Y,x)b^{-1}=H(D,bx).$$
  - Explanation: If a stabilizer lies in a conjugate parabolic, its conjugate is the stabilizer for the smaller type at the translated element.
- **claim-stabilizers-parabolic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stab-parabolic)): For every $Y\subseteq S$ and $x\in A$, there exist $R\subseteq Y$ and $c\in A_Y$ such that $$H(Y,x)=c^{-1}A_Rc.$$
  - Explanation: Every relative stabilizer is a conjugate of a standard parabolic subgroup, proved by induction on rank.
- **claim-initial-stabilizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stab-initial)): The relative family associated with $X$ satisfies $H(S,1)=A_X$.
  - Explanation: The stabilizer of the initial family is exactly the standard parabolic subgroup on X, which turns intersections into stabilizers.
- **claim-complete-parabolics** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-complete-parabolics)): Let $A=A_M$ with $S$ finite.* 1. *Every subgroup of $A$ with property $\mathrm{FA}'$ lies in a complete parabolic subgroup.* 2. *Every abstract homomorphism $L\to A$ from an almost-connected locally compact Hausdorff group has image in a complete parabolic.* 3. *If every complete standard parabolic of $A$ is lcH-slender, then $A$ is lcH-slender.
  - Explanation: Fixed-point and locally compact group consequences reduce to complete parabolic subgroups once parabolics are closed under intersection.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-classical",
        "exact_quote": "The group defined by the Artin presentation on $T\\subseteq S$ embeds in $A$, with image $A_T$. No sphericity hypothesis is needed here. For a spherical $T$, the positive Artin monoid $A_T^+$ embeds in $A_T$, is cancellative, and has greatest common divisors on both sides. Every element of $A_T$ is both a left fraction and a right fraction of positive elements. Any two elements $a,b\\in A_T$ thus have a common positive extension $pa=qb$, with $p,q\\in A_T^+$.*\n\n*Reduced expressions in any Coxeter system are related by braid moves. They therefore define a positive lift $\\iota(w)$ of a Coxeter element $w$. If $T$ is spherical and $w_T$ is its longest element, the left and right divisors of $\\Delta_T=\\iota(w_T)$ are precisely $\\{\\iota(w):w\\in W_T\\}$. In the finite geometric representation, root signs detect descents, the group acts simply transitively on chambers, and the translates of a closed fundamental chamber cover the entire dual space.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-action",
        "exact_quote": "There are bimodule chain homotopy equivalences $B_\\alpha D_\\alpha\\simeq Z\\simeq D_\\alpha B_\\alpha$. For a color $i\\in S$, define $$B_i=\\prod_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}B_\\alpha,\n \\qquad\n D_i=\\prod_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}D_\\alpha.$$ These products are independent of their order up to bimodule homotopy, are mutually inverse, and satisfy all the defining Artin relations. They define an action of $A$ by invertible graded bimodule homotopy classes, denoted $B_x$ for $x\\in A$. In particular, $$F_i(E):=B_i\\otimes_ZE\n  =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cone}}\\left(\n       \\bigoplus_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}\n       P_\\alpha\\otimes_\\mathbb Ce_\\alpha E\\longrightarrow E\\right).\n \\label{cat:color-evaluation}$$ Every $B_x$ preserves bounded projective complexes and preserves the cohomology of their bigraded Hom complexes. Its adjoint dual $B_x^\\vee$ represents its inverse. All the action identities are actual bimodule chain homotopy equivalences and remain valid after tensoring with any finite module complex, whether or not projective.*\n\n*For $H\\in\\mathscr C$, right tensoring of labels defines an additive functor $$T_H(P_{i,D})=P_{i,D\\otimes H}$$ on graded projectives. It preserves path lengths, extends to bounded homotopy categories, and commutes naturally up to homotopy with $B_x\\otimes_Z-$ for every $x\\in A$.",

      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are bimodule chain homotopy equivalences $B_\\alpha D_\\alpha\\simeq Z\\simeq D_\\alpha B_\\alpha$. For a color $i\\in S$, define $$B_i=\\prod_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}B_\\alpha,\n \\qquad\n D_i=\\prod_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}D_\\alpha.$$ These products are independent of their order up to bimodule homotopy, are mutually inverse, and satisfy all the defining Artin relations. They define an action of $A$ by invertible graded bimodule homotopy classes, denoted $B_x$ for $x\\in A$. In particular, $$F_i(E):=B_i\\otimes_ZE\n  =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cone}}\\left(\n       \\bigoplus_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}\n       P_\\alpha\\otimes_\\mathbb Ce_\\alpha E\\longrightarrow E\\right).\n \\label{cat:color-evaluation}$$ Every $B_x$ preserves bounded projective complexes and preserves the cohomology of their bigraded Hom complexes. Its adjoint dual $B_x^\\vee$ represents its inverse. All the action identities are actual bimodule chain homotopy equivalences and remain valid after tensoring with any finite module complex, whether or not projective.*\n\n*For $H\\in\\mathscr C$, right tensoring of labels defines an additive functor $$T_H(P_{i,D})=P_{i,D\\otimes H}$$ on graded projectives. It preserves path lengths, extends to bounded homotopy categories, and commutes naturally up to homotopy with $B_x\\otimes_Z-$ for every $x\\in A$.",
      "explanation": "Evaluation and coevaluation twists are mutually inverse bimodule homotopy equivalences satisfying the Artin relations, giving an action on bounded projective complexes compatible with label functors.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-family",
        "exact_quote": "There are nonnegative folded vectors $m^d(x)$ characterized by $$\\phi m^d(x)=\\sum_Lw_L\\dim Y^d(E_L(x)).\n \\label{cat:folded-counts}$$ They have finite level support. Each individual coordinate ranges over a locally finite subset of $\\mathbb R_{\\geq0}$. Weighted counts of tops and socles of the layers are likewise proportional within a color: a nonzero top or socle in that color occurs at every unfolded vertex of the color in some member of the family. The same proportionality holds for families of subobjects obtained functorially by twists and layer truncations.*\n\n*If only colors of $T\\subseteq S$ act, all generator spaces with their cochain and internal placements outside $\\widetilde T$ stay unchanged up to isomorphism. In particular, each $E_L(x)$ has exactly its original $f$-generator in placements $(0,0)$, and $$m_f^0(x)=1,\\qquad m_f^d(x)=0\\quad(d\\ne0).$$ Every $E_L(x)$ is indecomposable in the graded homotopy category.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lay-structure",
        "exact_quote": "The full subcategories defined by minimal generator levels at most $0$ and at least $0$ form a bounded $t$-structure. The truncation $\\tau_{\\le k}E$ is the subcomplex of levels at most $k$, and the complementary quotient is $\\tau_{\\ge k+1}E$.*\n\n*The heart is the abelian category of finite graded coefficient representations. In particular, subobjects of a pure layer are exactly its graded subrepresentations. Its unshifted cohomology layer at $d$ is $Y^d(E)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lay-extremal",
        "exact_quote": "If $M$ is pure at level $d$, then $$H^d(e_\\alpha M)\\cong\\mathop{\\mathrm{soc}}_\\alpha M,\n \\qquad\n H^{d+2}(e_\\alpha M)\\cong(\\mathop{\\mathrm{top}}_\\alpha M)\\langle2\\rangle.\n \\label{lay:extremal-formula}$$ The second shift records the token’s internal placement. More generally, if a minimal complex $E$ has no generators above $d$ at $\\alpha$ or its neighbors, its degree-$(d+2)$ test is the top of $Y^d(E)$ at $\\alpha$, shifted internally by $2$, and all higher test cohomology there vanishes. If there are no generators below $d$ at those vertices, its degree-$d$ test is the socle of $Y^d(E)$ there.*\n\n*A nonzero pure layer supported on a vertex set has a nonzero top and a nonzero socle somewhere in that set. The same assertions hold for right projective complexes, tested by $Me_\\alpha$.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lay-positive",
        "exact_quote": "Each $F_i$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$. For a pure input $M$ at $d$, its upper layer is supported on color $i$, has zero linear differential, and has multiplicity $\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{top}}_\\alpha M$ at each $\\alpha$ of that color. Its bottom layer has unchanged multiplicities at all other vertices, and $$\\dim Y^d(F_iM)_\\alpha\n =\\sum_\\beta(2I-\\widetilde C)_{\\alpha\\beta}\\dim M_\\beta\n       -\\dim M_\\alpha+\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{top}}_\\alpha M\n \\quad(\\alpha\\text{ of color }i).\n \\label{lay:positive-count}$$ The bottom layer has zero socle in color $i$.         "exact_quote": "If $h_J(E)$ is finite, then $$h_J(E)\\le h_J(B_\\alpha E)\\le h_J(E)+1.$$ Height increases exactly when $\\alpha$ was a highest-test vertex; in that case it is the only new highest-test vertex.         "exact_quote": "Let $b$ be a positive word in the unfolded finite-type generators. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **parabolic subgroup** ([source](#sec:intro)): A *parabolic subgroup* is a conjugate of some $A_X$.
- **spherical set** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For $T\subseteq S$, let $W_T$ denote the Coxeter group defined by the restricted matrix, and call $T$ *spherical* if $W_T$ is finite.
- **generator level** ([source](#cat:family-definition)): A generator in cochain placement $p$ and internal placement $a$ has *level* $p+a$.
- **cap** ([source](#stmt-cap-definition)): For $q\in\mathbb Z$ and a color set $D$, the family at $x$ is *capped at $q$ on $D$* if $$m_i^d(x)=0\qquad(i\in D,\ d>q).$$ Equivalently, every family member has no generator in those colors above level $q$. We write $\partial J$ for the exterior neighbors of $J$ in $\widehat S$.
- **candidate** ([source](#stmt-two-definition)): A candidate is $(J,A_Jz)$ with $J\subsetneq T$ and $z\in A_Tx_0$. Put $V=T\setminus J$, let $p$ harmonically interpolate $m^q(z)$ on $J$, and put $a=-(Cp)_T$. The requirements are: 1. the open colors $V$ are capped at $q$; 2. $a$ is strictly positive on $V$ and zero on $J$; 3. every representative $y\in A_Jz$ capped at $q$ on $T$ satisfies $m_J^q(y)\ge p_J$.
- **complete parabolic subgroup** ([source](#sec:fixed-point-consequences)): A parabolic subgroup is *complete* if it is conjugate to $A_T$ for a set $T$ with no infinite label; it need not be spherical.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-classical** [claim](#stmt-cat-classical) / [proof](#stmt-cat-classical): Recalled classical results on embedding, positive monoids and Coxeter chambers -> embedding and Garside tools on spherical sets
- **s-cartan** [claim](#stmt-cat-cartan) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-cartan): Neumann series for the inverse and a scaled quadratic form identity -> harmonic interpolation and null dichotomy
- **s-frobenius** [claim](#stmt-cat-frobenius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-frobenius): Semisimple trace pairing on arrows and tokens -> symmetric Frobenius trace and adjoint duality
- **s-unfolding** [claim](#stmt-cat-unfolding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-unfolding): Scaled form identity on unfolded blocks and chamber sign detection -> finite simply laced unfolding preserving descents
- **s-action** [claim](#stmt-cat-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-action): Cancel contractible pieces, twist conjugation and Matsumoto's theorem -> braid group action on projective complexes
- **s-family** [claim](#stmt-cat-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-family): Label tensoring, weighted multiplicities and exterior invariance -> folded counts and indecomposability
- **s-layer-structure** [claim](#stmt-lay-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-structure): Orthogonality of level ranges and identification of the heart -> bounded t-structure
- **s-adjacent** [claim](#stmt-lay-adjacent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-adjacent): Apply the functor to truncation triangles and read the layer sequence -> adjacent layer exact sequences
- **s-bottom** [claim](#stmt-lay-bottom) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-bottom): Discard higher quotient after each operation -> bottom-layer composition rule
- **s-extremal** [claim](#stmt-lay-extremal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-extremal): Path-length bookkeeping in the vertex test -> tests equal socle and shifted top
- **s-positive** [claim](#stmt-lay-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-positive): Evaluation cone cancellation and signed reflection count -> amplitude, multiplicity formula and test shift
- **s-inverse** [claim](#stmt-lay-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-inverse): Orthogonality characterisation of the t-structure applied to the inverse -> inverse amplitude
- **s-labels** [claim](#stmt-lay-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-labels): Exactness of label tensoring and weighted multiplicity identity -> proportionality on color fibers
- **s-height-change** [claim](#stmt-lay-height-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-height-change): Analyse the evaluation triangle at neighbors -> monotone height
- **s-top-divisor** [claim](#stmt-lay-top-divisor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-top-divisor): Induction on word length with a cokernel identification and the braid relation -> divisor of the word
- **s-reduced** [claim](#stmt-lay-reduced) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lay-reduced): At most one height increase along a reduced word -> amplitude of reduced lifts
- **s-capped-negative** [claim](#stmt-cap-capped-negative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-capped-negative): Truncation triangles with negative twists -> preservation of the joint cap
- **s-cap-access** [claim](#stmt-cap-access) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-access): Repeated negative letters chosen as Coxeter ascents -> representative capped on J
- **s-hull** [claim](#stmt-cap-hull) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-hull): Twist, truncate and untwist for each Weyl element, then separate in the chamber cover -> convex hull description
- **s-duality** [claim](#stmt-two-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-duality): Minimize the bimodule complex and apply adjoint duality -> duality of generator counts
- **s-greedy** [claim](#stmt-two-greedy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-greedy): Greatest simple suffix argument and unfolding -> descent of divisibility to colors
- **s-detection** [claim](#stmt-two-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-detection): Split off the residual simple and peel common final colors -> parabolic membership detector
- **s-negative-bound** [claim](#stmt-two-negative-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-negative-bound): Cancel offending inserted terms simultaneously -> preserved normalized bound
- **s-tensor** [claim](#stmt-two-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-tensor): Filter the tensor complex by middle path length -> surviving middle homology
- **s-normalization** [claim](#stmt-two-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-normalization): Positive moves that remove highest right tops, terminating by sphericity -> normalized representative
- **s-subobject-tensor** [claim](#stmt-two-subobject-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-subobject-tensor): Substitute incoming images using dual arrows -> vanishing top homology for subfamilies
- **s-euler** [claim](#stmt-two-euler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-euler): Linearity of the Euler characteristic over the hull -> lower bound for middle homology
- **s-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-two-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-euler-2): Compare two candidates with the Euler estimate and detect the coset -> uniqueness of the typed coset
- **s-hom** [claim](#stmt-null-hom) / [proof](#proof-stmt-null-hom): Supertrace pairing and filtration by path length -> pairings and middle vanishing
- **s-injection** [claim](#stmt-null-injection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-null-injection): Secondary differential excluded by the cap -> injection of Hom classes
- **s-separation** [claim](#stmt-null-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-null-separation): Affine inequality on the hull and Euler identity -> boundary and Cartan vanishing
- **s-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-null-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-null-separation-2): Upward induction over levels firing colors of J until the boundary is empty -> impossibility of strict excess
- **s-vertex-filtration** [claim](#stmt-ht-vertex-filtration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-vertex-filtration): Insert vertices in order and track components -> one root per component
- **s-sublevels** [claim](#stmt-ht-sublevels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-sublevels): Induction on size with an ordering of vertices and the filtration lemma -> connected sublevels
- **s-well-order** [claim](#stmt-ht-well-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-well-order): Finite alphabet with finite initial segments and level-by-level descent -> well-ordered heights
- **s-upper** [claim](#stmt-ht-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-upper): Compare at the highest incidence level using the Cartan lemma -> upper neighbors spherical and connected
- **s-lower** [claim](#stmt-ht-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-lower): Eligible-color analysis and product of block factors -> lower neighbors characterised and connected
- **s-descending** [claim](#stmt-ht-descending) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-descending): Join upper and lower parts through comparability -> connected descending neighborhoods
- **s-ties** [claim](#stmt-ht-ties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-ties): Compare recorded vectors and sign masks across a tie -> tie compatibility
- **s-minima** [claim](#stmt-ht-minima) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-minima): Replace maximal tied segments by paths in the strict sublevel -> connected minimum set
- **s-containment** [claim](#stmt-ht-containment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-containment): Translate a minimum by a stabilizer element and follow a minimum path -> stabilizer containment
- **s-path-bound** [claim](#stmt-sph-path-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-path-bound): Projection argument on path spaces using a positive eigenvector -> nilpotence bound
- **s-cochain-drop** [claim](#stmt-sph-cochain-drop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-cochain-drop): Long exact sequence of the evaluation triangle -> drop of cochain bound
- **s-empty-placement** [claim](#stmt-sph-empty-placement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-empty-placement): Remove layers from the top using spanning by incoming arrow images -> generator-free window
- **s-no-chain** [claim](#stmt-sph-no-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-no-chain): Contradiction with indecomposability of a transported projective -> no bounded chain
- **s-common-extension** [claim](#stmt-sph-common-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-common-extension): Cancel common left atoms and use the top-divisor lemma -> height-bounded common extension
- **s-proper-support** [claim](#stmt-sph-proper-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-proper-support): Argue by contradiction, building an infinite chain -> proper support set
- **s-sph-containment** [claim](#stmt-sph-containment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-containment): Apply common extension to p and ps, or contract acyclic evaluation terms -> dichotomy for spherical stabilizers
- **s-conjugation** [claim](#stmt-stab-conjugation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-conjugation): Invertibility of the action -> conjugated stabilizer
- **s-stabilizer-parabolic** [claim](#stmt-stab-parabolic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-parabolic): Induction on rank splitting spherical and nonspherical cases -> parabolic stabilizers
- **s-initial** [claim](#stmt-stab-initial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-initial): Identify the empty-type vertex as a minimum and apply containment -> initial stabilizer equals A_X
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-initial-2): Reduce to an intersection with a conjugate and identify it with a stabilizer -> parabolic intersections
- **s-arbitrary** [claim](#stmt-cor-arbitrary-intersections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-initial-3): Iterate strict intersection steps, each decreasing the defining set -> finite witness subfamily
- **s-nonclique** [claim](#stmt-cor-nonclique-dynamics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-initial-4): Apply a published visual splitting criterion using parabolic intersections -> hyperbolicity and trivial intersection witness
- **s-complete** [claim](#stmt-cor-complete-parabolics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-complete-parabolics): Invoke published results for groups with parabolics closed under intersection -> reduction to complete parabolics
- **s-membership** [claim](#stmt-cor-membership) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-complete-parabolics-2): Exact arithmetic over a cyclotomic field and minimization of a transported projective -> decision procedure

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-harmonic-extension-residual**: For a spherical color set T, prescribed exterior coordinates have a unique extension p with (Cp)_T = 0, and this extension is obtained by solving the positive definite system on T.
- **inv-harmonic-extension-nonneg**: The harmonic extension is nonnegative whenever the prescribed exterior coordinates are nonnegative.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-nonspherical-interpolation**: Apply the harmonic interpolation to a color set that is not spherical, for example one containing an affine or indefinite diagram. -> The restricted Cartan matrix is no longer positive definite, so the extension need not exist or be unique, and the routine reports a non-spherical input instead of a vector.
- **anti-negative-exterior-data**: Expect a nonnegative interpolated vector from exterior data that has negative entries. -> Nonnegativity is only guaranteed for nonnegative prescribed coordinates, so interpolated entries may be negative.
- **anti-efficiency-claim**: Treat this small interpolation routine as a performance-certified solver for the full membership or word problem. -> The source gives a decision procedure without any complexity bound, and the interpolation routine is only one linear-algebra ingredient of the framework.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The harmonic interpolation on a spherical color set is a finite exact linear-algebra computation. The check is restricted to integer Coxeter labels 2 and 3, where the Cartan entries are rational; it illustrates one lemma and is not the full membership algorithm.

```python
# INPUTS: n, the 0/1 adjacency matrix b (n rows), k, the k indices of T, then n rational values p (entries inside T are ignored).
# PRECONDITIONS: b symmetric with zero diagonal, entries 0 or 1 (Coxeter labels 2 or 3); T lists distinct indices.
# OUTPUTS: the n harmonic-extended values as space separated fractions, or the word NONSPHERICAL if the Cartan matrix on T is not positive definite.
# TIME: cubic in k for the elimination plus quadratic work to build the right-hand side.
# SPACE: quadratic in n.
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with Fraction, no floating point.
# FAILURE: a non-positive pivot in the elimination reports NONSPHERICAL; malformed input raises an exception.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    n = int(data[0])
    b = [[int(data[1 + i * n + j]) for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]
    pos = 1 + n * n
    k = int(data[pos])
    pos += 1
    T = [int(x) for x in data[pos:pos + k]]
    pos += k
    p = [Fraction(x) for x in data[pos:pos + n]]
    Tset = set(T)
    A = [[(2 if r == c else 0) - b[T[r]][T[c]] for c in range(k)] for r in range(k)]
    A = [[Fraction(v) for v in row] for row in A]
    rhs = []
    for r in range(k):
        s = Fraction(0)
        for e in range(n):
            if e not in Tset:
                s += b[T[r]][e] * p[e]
        rhs.append(s)
    for i in range(k):
        if A[i][i] <= 0:
            print("NONSPHERICAL")
            return
        for j in range(i + 1, k):
            f = A[j][i] / A[i][i]
            if f != 0:
                for c in range(i, k):
                    A[j][c] -= f * A[i][c]
                rhs[j] -= f * rhs[i]
    x = [Fraction(0)] * k
    for i in range(k - 1, -1, -1):
        s = rhs[i]
        for c in range(i + 1, k):
            s -= A[i][c] * x[c]
        x[i] = s / A[i][i]
    out = list(p)
    for r in range(k):
        out[T[r]] = x[r]
    print(" ".join(str(v) for v in out))


main()
```

A library can expose it as a single decision function for parabolic membership and the word problem, with exact arithmetic over a computable cyclotomic field. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No simulation dynamics, physics or interactive state appears; the results are structural theorems about subgroups of Artin groups. |

| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The proof of the membership corollary spells out finite steps: build the algebra with exact coefficients, transport a framing projective by the twists of a word, minimise by Gaussian elimination, and test a single generator. The stabiliser bound in the arbitrary-intersection corollary also gives a greedy loop that selects a witness subfamily. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proposes no storage layout or index; graded complexes appear only as proof objects, so no reusable data structure is claimed. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No parallelism, memory hierarchy or hardware consideration appears in the text. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper makes no hardness or security statement. Decidability of the word problem is a worst-case solvability result, not a statement about protocol security. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning, statistics or data in the paper; the categorical weights are quantum-dimension style quantities, not model parameters. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimisation problem is posed. The harmonic height is an ordering device for a connectivity argument, not a quantity to be minimised in practice. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, replication or consensus content appears in the paper. |
| other (computational group theory) | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Parabolic closure of a subset, the lattice of parabolic subgroups under intersection, and trivial-intersection conjugates for proper parabolics in irreducible groups with an infinite label are structural facts a group-theory system could use to cross-check computations. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-finite-generating-set** (hypothesis): The intersection theorem is stated only for a Coxeter matrix on a finite generating set. The reduction to at most \|S\| steps and the categorical construction both rely on that finiteness. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to Artin groups with infinitely many generators', 'Using the witness-size bound when the generating set is not finite']
- **limit-no-complexity-bound** (formal_scope): The membership and word-problem procedure is only shown to decide the question. The paper gives no running-time or space bound for it. Blocked use: ['Advertising the procedure as polynomial-time or real-time', 'Using it where a performance guarantee is required']
- **limit-nonclique-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The acylindrical hyperbolicity and trivial-intersection conclusions need an irreducible matrix and at least one infinite label. Reducible matrices, or matrices with only finite labels, are outside the corollary. Blocked use: ['Claiming a conjugate meeting a proper parabolic trivially when the matrix is reducible', 'Claiming acylindrical hyperbolicity when every label is finite']
- **limit-positive-fractions-spherical-only** (edge_case): Positive fractions and Garside-type divisor tools are available only inside spherical subgroups. They cannot be assumed for the ambient group or for nonspherical parabolics, so the nonspherical case needs the height argument. Blocked use: ['Using positive normal forms or gcds directly in a nonspherical parabolic', 'Assuming common positive extensions in the ambient group']
- **limit-framed-action-faithfulness** (formal_scope): The faithfulness obtained for X empty concerns the framed, per-edge-labelled algebra built in this paper. It does not settle the question for the original unframed construction. Blocked use: ['Transferring the faithfulness conclusion to the original unframed action', 'Citing it as resolving the unframed faithfulness question']
- **limit-cap-needed-for-uniqueness** (hypothesis): Two-point uniqueness of the typed coset requires the family to be capped on the boundary of the spherical set at the given level. Without the cap the candidate comparison is not available. Blocked use: ['Invoking uniqueness of candidates for an uncapped coset', 'Choosing the level q without checking the boundary cap']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 254 (`lean/docs/254.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Harmonic heights and the Artin $K(\pi,1)$ conjecture](../../preprints/Harmonic-heights-and-the-Artin-K-pi-1-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [An Artin group with no geometric $\mathrm{CAT}(0)$ action](../../preprints/An-Artin-group-with-no-geometric-CAT-0-action-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Parabolic Intersections in Artin Groups](../../preprints/Parabolic-intersections-in-Artin-groups-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Artin $K(\pi,1)$ conjecture asserts that the standard Salvetti complex of every finite-rank Artin group is aspherical. The formalized result proves the equivalent statement that its universal cover is contractible.
>
> It applies to every Coxeter matrix on a finite generating set, including empty and disconnected diagrams and infinite edge labels. The associated Coxeter and Artin groups may be infinite. Later corollaries in the manuscript are not included.
>
> The geometric-action question asks whether every Artin group acts properly and cocompactly by isometries on a proper CAT(0) space. The formalized result gives a counterexample: an Artin group defined by a symmetric matrix on $116$ generators, with diagonal entries $1$ and off-diagonal entries in $\{2,3,\infty\}$.
>
> It admits no such action on any nonempty proper CAT(0) space, in any dimension.
>
> The formalization proves that the intersection of any family of parabolic subgroups of a finite-rank Artin group is parabolic. Every such intersection is already the intersection of at most the rank many members, and every subset has a unique parabolic closure. Both finite and infinite Coxeter labels are allowed.
>
> For irreducible Artin groups with an infinite Coxeter label, it also proves acylindrical hyperbolicity and weak malnormality of every proper parabolic subgroup. These are the intersection and structural consequences selected from the paper.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Parabolic intersections, parabolic closure, and non-clique Artin-group dynamics. -/

namespace HarmonicArtin

universe u

variable {S : Type u}

def alternating (s t : S) : ℕ → FreeGroup S
  | 0 => 1
  | n + 1 => FreeGroup.of s * alternating t s n

def braidRelators (M : CoxeterMatrix S) : Set (FreeGroup S) :=
  Set.range fun st : S × S =>
    alternating st.1 st.2 (M st.1 st.2) *
      (alternating st.2 st.1 (M st.1 st.2))⁻¹

abbrev Artin (M : CoxeterMatrix S) := PresentedGroup (braidRelators M)

def generator (M : CoxeterMatrix S) (s : S) : Artin M := PresentedGroup.of s

def artinParabolic (M : CoxeterMatrix S) (T : Set S) : Subgroup (Artin M) :=
  Subgroup.closure (generator M '' T)

end HarmonicArtin

namespace HarmonicArtin.ParabolicIntersections

variable {G : Type*} [Group G]

/-- `g P g⁻¹`, with membership expressed without existential witnesses. -/
def conjugate (g : G) (P : Subgroup G) : Subgroup G :=
  P.comap (MulAut.conj g⁻¹).toMonoidHom

variable {S : Type*} (M : CoxeterMatrix S)

/-- Conjugates of standard subgroups, with no spherical-type restriction. -/
def IsParabolic (P : Subgroup (Artin M)) : Prop :=
  ∃ (X : Set S) (g : Artin M), P = conjugate g (artinParabolic M X)

end HarmonicArtin.ParabolicIntersections

namespace HarmonicArtin.ParabolicIntersections

noncomputable def gromovProduct {X : Type} [MetricSpace X] (o x y : X) : ℝ :=
  (dist o x + dist o y - dist x y)/2

def IsGromovHyperbolic (X : Type) [MetricSpace X] : Prop :=
  ∃ δ : ℝ, 0 ≤ δ ∧ ∀ o x y z : X,
    min (gromovProduct o x y) (gromovProduct o y z) ≤ gromovProduct o x z + δ

def IsGeodesicMetric (X : Type) [MetricSpace X] : Prop :=
  ∀ x y : X, ∃ γ : Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) (dist x y) → X,
    Isometry γ ∧ γ ⟨0,le_rfl,dist_nonneg⟩=x ∧ γ ⟨dist x y,dist_nonneg,le_rfl⟩=y

def IsGromovSequence {X : Type} [MetricSpace X] (o : X) (u : ℕ → X) : Prop :=
  ∀ R : ℝ, ∃ N : ℕ, ∀ m, N ≤ m → ∀ n, N ≤ n → R ≤ gromovProduct o (u m) (u n)

def IsAcylindrical (G X : Type) [Group G] [MetricSpace X] [MulAction G X] : Prop :=
  ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ R : ℝ, ∃ N : ℕ, ∀ x y : X, R ≤ dist x y →
    {g : G | dist x (g • x) ≤ ε ∧ dist y (g • y) ≤ ε}.Finite ∧
      {g : G | dist x (g • x) ≤ ε ∧ dist y (g • y) ≤ ε}.ncard ≤ N

def HasThreeOrbitLimitPoints (G X : Type) [Group G] [MetricSpace X] [MulAction G X] : Prop :=
  ∃ o : X, ∃ u : Fin 3 → ℕ → G,
    (∀ i, IsGromovSequence o (fun n => u i n • o)) ∧
    ∀ i j, i ≠ j → ∃ B : ℝ, ∀ m n, gromovProduct o (u i m • o) (u j n • o) ≤ B

/-- An isometric non-elementary acylindrical action on a geodesic
Gromov-hyperbolic metric space. -/
def AcylindricallyHyperbolic (G : Type) [Group G] : Prop :=
  ∃ (X : Type) (metric : MetricSpace X) (action : MulAction G X),
    @IsGeodesicMetric X metric ∧ @IsGromovHyperbolic X metric ∧
    (∀ g : G, @Isometry X X metric.toPseudoMetricSpace.toPseudoEMetricSpace metric.toPseudoMetricSpace.toPseudoEMetricSpace
      (fun x => @SMul.smul G X action.toSMul g x)) ∧
    @IsAcylindrical G X _ metric action ∧ @HasThreeOrbitLimitPoints G X _ metric action

def WeaklyMalnormal {G : Type} [Group G] (P : Subgroup G) : Prop :=
  ∃ g : G, (↑(P ⊓ conjugate g P) : Set G).Finite

variable {S : Type} (M : CoxeterMatrix S)

/-- No nontrivial partition has every cross label equal to two. -/
def IsIrreducible : Prop :=
  ∀ Y : Set S, Y.Nonempty → Yᶜ.Nonempty → ∃ s ∈ Y, ∃ t ∉ Y, M s t ≠ 2

end HarmonicArtin.ParabolicIntersections

namespace HarmonicArtin.ParabolicIntersections

variable {S : Type} [Fintype S] [LinearOrder S] (M : CoxeterMatrix S)

/-- The intersection of two conjugates of standard parabolics is parabolic. -/
theorem unconditional_parabolic_intersections
    (X Y : Set S) (g h : Artin M) :
    ∃ (Z : Set S) (k : Artin M),
      conjugate g (artinParabolic M X) ⊓ conjugate h (artinParabolic M Y) =
        conjugate k (artinParabolic M Z) := by
  sorry

/-- An arbitrary parabolic intersection is obtained from at most the rank many members. -/
theorem unconditional_arbitrary_intersections
    (F : Set (Subgroup (Artin M))) (hF : ∀ P ∈ F, IsParabolic M P) :
    (∃ F₀ : Finset (Subgroup (Artin M)),
      (↑F₀ : Set (Subgroup (Artin M))) ⊆ F ∧ F₀.card ≤ Nat.card S ∧
        sInf F = sInf (↑F₀ : Set (Subgroup (Artin M)))) ∧ IsParabolic M (sInf F) := by
  sorry

/-- Every subset is contained in a unique smallest parabolic subgroup. -/
theorem unconditional_parabolic_closure (E : Set (Artin M)) :
    ∃! P : Subgroup (Artin M), IsParabolic M P ∧ E ⊆ P ∧
      ∀ Q : Subgroup (Artin M), IsParabolic M Q → E ⊆ Q → P ≤ Q := by
  sorry

/-- An irreducible non-clique Artin group has an acylindrical non-elementary
hyperbolic action and all its proper parabolics are weakly malnormal. -/
theorem unconditional_nonclique_dynamics
    (hIrred : IsIrreducible M) (hInf : ∃ s t : S, s ≠ t ∧ M s t = 0) :
    AcylindricallyHyperbolic (Artin M) ∧
      ∀ P : Subgroup (Artin M), IsParabolic M P → P ≠ ⊤ → WeaklyMalnormal P := by
  sorry

end HarmonicArtin.ParabolicIntersections

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every intersection of parabolic subgroups of any finite-rank Artin group, with arbitrary finite or infinite Coxeter labels, is parabolic. This resolves the Parabolic Intersection Conjecture affirmatively.

<a id="sec:intro"></a>
### Introduction

Let $S$ be finite, and let $M=(m_{st})_{s,t\in S}$ be a symmetric Coxeter matrix. Thus $m_{ss}=1$ and, for $s\ne t$, $m_{st}\in\{2,3,\ldots\}\cup\{\infty\}$. The associated Artin group is $$A=A_M=\left\langle \sigma_s\ (s\in S)\ \middle|\
 \underbrace{\sigma_s\sigma_t\sigma_s\cdots}_{m_{st}}
 =\underbrace{\sigma_t\sigma_s\sigma_t\cdots}_{m_{st}}
 \quad(m_{st}<\infty)\right\rangle.$$ For $X\subseteq S$, write $A_X=\langle\sigma_s:s\in X\rangle$, with $A_\varnothing=\{1\}$. A *parabolic subgroup* is a conjugate of some $A_X$. The rank here refers to the cardinality of the defining set; no finiteness assumption on the associated Coxeter group is understood. The Coxeter group is obtained by imposing $\sigma_s^2=1$ for all $s$; a type, or a parabolic of that type, is called *spherical* when its Coxeter group is finite.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Parabolic intersections). *For every Coxeter matrix $M$ on a finite set $S$, subsets $X,Y\subseteq S$, and $g,h\in A_M$, there are $Z\subseteq S$ and $k\in A_M$ such that $$gA_Xg^{-1}\cap hA_Yh^{-1}=kA_Zk^{-1}.$$*

</div>

In particular, the result applies to arbitrary conjugates of nonspherical parabolics and to diagrams with infinite labels. The passage from pairwise intersections to arbitrary intersections and parabolic closure is established in (Möller, Paris, and Varghese 2024, Proposition 1.5 and Corollary 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[MoellerParisVarghese2024](#ref-MoellerParisVarghese2024)]. Our inside-parabolic description gives the following form, whose proof in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">8.1</a> also records a finite witness bound.

<a id="stmt-cor-arbitrary-intersections"></a>
<div id="cor:arbitrary-intersections" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Arbitrary intersections and parabolic closure). *For every family $\mathcal F$ of parabolic subgroups of $A$, there is a subfamily $\mathcal F_0\subseteq\mathcal F$ with $|\mathcal F_0|\le |S|$ such that $$\bigcap_{P\in\mathcal F}P=\bigcap_{P\in\mathcal F_0}P.$$ Here the intersection of an empty family is $A$. In particular, arbitrary intersections of parabolic subgroups are parabolic, and every subset $E\subseteq A$ is contained in a unique smallest parabolic subgroup.*

</div>

Writing $\operatorname{PC}(E)$ for this parabolic closure, the parabolic subgroups form a complete lattice: their meet is intersection and the join of a family is the parabolic closure of its union.

The same stabilizer description gives an effective membership test. Here the conjugating element and the tested element are supplied as words in the standard generators and their inverses.

<a id="stmt-cor-membership"></a>
<div id="cor:membership" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Word and parabolic membership problems). *There is an algorithm which, given a finite Coxeter matrix $M$, a subset $X\subseteq S$, and words $u,v$ in the standard generators of $A_M$ and their inverses, decides whether the element represented by $v$ belongs to $uA_Xu^{-1}$. In particular, the word problem is decidable for every finite-rank Artin group.*

</div>

The proof in Section <a href="#sec:algorithms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algorithms">8.3</a> constructs the finite algebra with exact algebraic coefficients and tests a single transported projective by Gaussian elimination.

The intersection theorem also supplies the hypothesis of a published criterion for Artin groups with a visual splitting. We state the result with the resulting trivial-intersection witness; its proof is in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">8.1</a>.

<a id="stmt-cor-nonclique-dynamics"></a>
<div id="cor:nonclique-dynamics" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (Non-clique Artin-group dynamics). *Suppose $M$ is irreducible, meaning that there is no nontrivial partition $S=S_1\sqcup S_2$ with $m_{st}=2$ for every $s\in S_1$, $t\in S_2$, and suppose $m_{st}=\infty$ for some pair. Then $A$ is acylindrically hyperbolic. Every proper parabolic subgroup $P$ has a conjugate meeting it trivially: there is $g\in A$ such that $P\cap gPg^{-1}=\{1\}$.*

</div>

The acylindrical-hyperbolicity conclusion in this entire non-clique scope already appears in (Kato and Oguni 2025, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[KatoOguni2025](#ref-KatoOguni2025)]. Corollary <a href="#cor:nonclique-dynamics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:nonclique-dynamics">4</a> records the deduction through the parabolic-intersection criterion, not a new acylindrical-hyperbolicity class.

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> also removes the intersection hypothesis from the fixed-point and locally compact group results of (Möller, Paris, and Varghese 2024) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[MoellerParisVarghese2024](#ref-MoellerParisVarghese2024)]; their precise consequences are stated in Section <a href="#sec:fixed-point-consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fixed-point-consequences">8.2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and method

Standard parabolic subgroups retain the Artin presentation of their submatrix: this unrestricted embedding theorem is due to van der Lek (Lek 1983) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[VanDerLek1983](#ref-VanDerLek1983)], with subsequent treatments in (Paris 1997; Charney and Paris 2014) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Paris1997](#ref-Paris1997), [CharneyParis2014](#ref-CharneyParis2014)]. Charney and Paris also prove convexity of standard parabolics in the standard word metric (Charney and Paris 2014, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CharneyParis2014](#ref-CharneyParis2014)]. Blufstein and Paris prove the general nesting theorem: an ambient parabolic subgroup contained in a parabolic subgroup $Q$ is parabolic as a subgroup of $Q$ (Blufstein and Paris 2023, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BlufsteinParis2023](#ref-BlufsteinParis2023)]. The intersection problem asks whether the intersection of two arbitrary ambient parabolics is itself parabolic. It is the Parabolic Intersection Conjecture; precise formulations appear in (Godelle 2023, Conjecture 1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Godelle2023](#ref-Godelle2023)] and (Cumplido 2026, sec. 3, Conjecture 4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Cumplido2026Survey](#ref-Cumplido2026Survey)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves it affirmatively.

Several approaches established this closure in substantial classes. For right-angled Artin groups, Duncan, Kazachkov and Remeslennikov proved even the arbitrary-family intersection statement (Duncan, Kazachkov, and Remeslennikov 2007, Proposition 2.8) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DuncanKazachkovRemeslennikov2007](#ref-DuncanKazachkovRemeslennikov2007)]. In spherical type, Cumplido, Gebhardt, González-Meneses and Wiest proved pairwise closure using Garside theory and parabolic closures (Cumplido et al. 2019, Theorem 9.5) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CumplidoEtAl2019](#ref-CumplidoEtAl2019)]. Their argument builds on the finite-type positive-monoid structure developed by Brieskorn and Saito and by Deligne (Brieskorn and Saito 1972; Deligne 1972) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BrieskornSaito1972](#ref-BrieskornSaito1972), [Deligne1972](#ref-Deligne1972)]. Such positive fractions are available inside spherical subgroups; they are not available as a general premise for the ambient groups considered here.

Geometric methods provide a different route. Cumplido, Martin and Vaskou obtained arbitrary intersections in large type through fixed paths in the Artin complex and stabilizer induction (Cumplido, Martin, and Vaskou 2023, Theorem A and Theorem 11) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CumplidoMartinVaskou2023](#ref-CumplidoMartinVaskou2023)]. Blufstein extended this approach to $(2,2)$-free two-dimensional Artin groups (Blufstein 2022, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Blufstein2022](#ref-Blufstein2022)]. Haettel proved the result for affine types $\widetilde A$ and $\widetilde C$ using geodesic bicombings (Haettel 2024, Corollary 8.3) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Haettel2024](#ref-Haettel2024)]; Cumplido, Gavazzi and Paris later gave a short alternative proof in type $\widetilde A$ (Cumplido, Gavazzi, and Paris 2024, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CumplidoGavazziParis2024](#ref-CumplidoGavazziParis2024)].

The distinction between the ambient group and the two intersected subgroups is especially important in FC type.
